- Resolved: the four button slots are the mash detector: Triangle 0x0010, Circle 0x0020, Cross 0x0040 and Square 0x0080 on the pressed-edge word (Behaviors 6 and 9B). The attack path uses R2 or Circle directly.
- Resolved: **Square handlers.** One chooser handles every Square press, with a fixed order and a branch per special state; it is described in SPEC-0022 (Interaction prompts: chooser order and precedence). Talk starts the talker's callback thread, a script door starts its callback (SPEC-0006), the item ask-about starts a callback (SPEC-0030), Buy runs the purchase (SPEC-0050), Read Book takes the pending book (SPEC-0028), and the chest press is a script thread outside the chooser (SPEC-0048). The chooser never uses an item: item use comes only from the quick-item panel (Behavior 1A, rule 7; SPEC-0021) and the item screen (SPEC-0035). High.
- Answered: button roles. Behavior 1A (Cross jump, L1 shield guard, L2 strafe, Triangle quick-item panel, R1 run).
- Resolved: stick mode at attack start (Behavior 52; SPEC-0036, Attack router, Start). The four entry variants are the four rows of that start rule. The variant flag P1 is not set by a character type: it is the party's shared weapon-ready word, one word that both heroes read, so the clips with the weapon out (ids 7 to 11, 1 and the long idles) follow whichever hero is active. It is set to 1 by an attack press when the active hero's first weapon-list entry exists and is not empty (the same step also sets the shield-ready word), by the first hit taken while it is clear (SPEC-0036, Taking a hit), by the air attack routines of both heroes, and by the script call SetHeroEquippedState with true; it is cleared by the Square weapon-away, the talk state (25), state 28, SwitchHero and several other hero routines (SPEC-0030, SetHeroEquippedState). High for the sites; Medium-High for the exact press condition.
- Resolved: state 53 is the carried-hero state; it suppresses rebuilding the drawn copy's travel step and basis during the carry (SPEC-0005 Attach points and carrying; SPEC-0040 Behaviors 3 and 14C). The height-follow movement predicate passes only for hero states 7 to 11 and 43 to 46 (High for the accepted states; Medium-High for their interpretation as the jump exception).
- Resolved: the catch-up branch is selected using the flag value at function entry. When the flag is off, any threshold that sets it (including g < −0.5 or counter > 120) finishes through the idle path that invocation: speed is set to 0 and displayed height does not move. The first catch-up step runs on the next motion-record update for both falls and rises (High for branch and tick order). Vectors 10 and 12 cover the switch. The drawn-copy position starts equal to world position at record construction, and position setters also refresh it. The camera refresh path receives the drawn-copy position; a transform path reads it as well, though that path's subsystem is not identified. Still open: whether all character rendering uses the copy or separately reads world position.
- Height follow constants: 0.05, 0.1, 0.5, 3.5 and 120 are fixed constants. Matching data words exist elsewhere in the boot data but none is tied to this routine.
- Height follow consumers: the camera refresh path receives the hero motion record's drawn-copy position. In the recovered direct-reference inventory, that camera refresh is the only identified caller of the position accessor. A separate transform routine reads a four-component value at the same offset through a nested object, but the evidence does not tie that nested field to the hero motion record or to the ordinary character-model draw path. Thus the camera handoff is established; whether the character model or every render path consumes the drawn copy remains open. The ground probe and ground constraint update world position; the height-follow routine separately writes displayed height to the drawn copy. The movement predicate's accepted hero states are 7 to 11 and 43 to 46; its design intent and use by other callers remain unknown.
- Answered: states 31 and 42. They have no route and are never written by hero code (Behavior 3, route table).
- Hero walk and run speed and acceleration: resolved (Behavior 5; no acceleration beyond the one-pass lag). Resolved: jump and fall are Behaviors 9A, 9B and 50, climb Behaviors 13 and 51; no swim rule is known.
- Resolved: **Behavior 13 versus Behavior 3.** Behavior 13 is the sub-mode machine of the ladder state 41; its sub-mode word (+0x9BC) is shared with the climb state 21. It is not the action state of Behavior 3, and its animations 90 to 95 are ladder clips.
- Resolved: **ladder sub-modes 1 and 6 and the 0.45 / 1.25 loop.** Sub-mode 1 is the mount and sub-mode 6 a rung step down (request 95); the loop finds the nearest rung, the rungs being 1.25 apart from 0.45 (Behavior 13).
- Resolved: **ladder thresholds.** 0.9 and 0.8 are the mount targets, 0.5 the climb-down floor, 0.99 the rung-step progress gate, -0.5 and 2.0 the jump-off push and lift; 1.3 and 1.27 are the top tests of rung A and rung B, in units (Behavior 13).
- Resolved: **cue 140** plays once per rung step and with the mount-from-above clip 120 (Behavior 13).
- Resolved: **Landing test (Behavior 14), both kind-4/7 arms.** It is the hero's landing break on world breakables, not a general landing test. V30, V60 and s are the breakable volume's base point, axis and length; the positive-shape arm is the one-face footprint query, the zero-or-negative arm is the shifted-field footprint query (never present on disc), and both end in the top gate Q.y − 1.5 below the hero y (Behavior 14; SPEC-0036; High).
- Resolved: **Behavior 16 gravity input.** Behavior 16 is a scripted jump onto a box that is never run. Its start test uses g = 32 as stated; the speed it waits on is the record velocity (+0x60); the fall law is in SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A item 7.
- Resolved: **Behavior 16 units.** v0 = sqrt(2 g h) is in record velocity units; at gain 1.0 it rises by about h. The routine is never run.
- Resolved: **Behavior 16 terms.** h is the box top minus the hero's feet plus 2.0; the candidates are the hero's cell trigger boxes of kind 4 or 7 hit by a segment query (flag bits 0x0004 and 0x0002 skip a box); the 6.0 vector is 6.0 times the flattened facing.
- Resolved: **block list (Behavior 15).** The 0xB0-byte records are the movable blocks built from the world's type-11 table (SPEC-0006, SPEC-0048); the mode value 0 to 3 is the block face index; 0.5 is the facing dot bound; the 0.2 and 3.5 bounds are strict.
- Resolved: **Action start gate (Behavior 17).** The spatial exclusion formulas and their mode/index selectors are resolved in Behavior 17 (High). The field cleared on success is the action record's elapsed-step counter (+0x90); it advances once per active update against the stored duration byte (+0x91), then the active word is cleared at completion.
- Resolved: **Jump-action helpers (Behavior 17(b)).** They are the climb corner sub-modes 11 (animation 51) and 12 (animation 50) of hero state 21, so a hero path exists; the "state 13 or 2" they set is the climb sub-mode 13 or 2, not state 13 of Behavior 3 (Behavior 17(b); High).
- Resolved: **Behavior 18** is the Miamouse carry of state 57; sub-states 0 to 3 are pick up, stand, walk and put down (High). In this routine the hold begins on the second tick of sub-state 0; this matches SPEC-0029 Behavior 28 (High).
- **Target pick (Behavior 19).** Resolved in part: the picker accepts only class-21 Miamice, then requires their +0xC84 catchable word to be nonzero. SPEC-0029 Behavior 28 establishes that this word is set while idle/wandering and cleared while fleeing, released/landing or delivered/caged. The gate meaning is established for this class-21 picker only. The range is a strict Euclidean 3-D distance under 4.0 units. The alignment vector is candidate position minus hero position; after normalization it is dotted with the hero motion record's forward row B3 (+0x2B0), requiring a result strictly above 0.5. Thus qualifying candidates lie within a strict forward cone narrower than 120 degrees. **[High]**
- Resolved: **Square-selector target cascade (Behavior 19, 22A and 22).** The ground Square selector tries the state-21 actor pick first; success takes its interaction branch and skips the other finders. On failure it tests the wall-panel grab (Behavior 22A); success stores the panel and sub-mode 1 and requests state 21. Only wall-panel failure reaches the ladder test (Behavior 22); success stores the ladder and sub-mode 1 and takes the ladder transition. If all three fail, other Square-action tests continue. The wall-panel and ladder finders are not the SPEC-0036 combat target picker. High for order and branch conditions; Medium for outcome naming.
- **Hero reference vectors (Behavior 19).** The helpers that give the hero vectors used by the alignment test of Behavior 19 are not described (Low-Medium). Resolved for Behavior 22: the fallback direction for a centred stick is the facing.
- Resolved: **Behavior 22 third window.** It is the height window -1.0 <= y <= LV - (H + 1.25) along the ladder's up axis, with the upper limit LV in state 30.
- Resolved: **Air and landing states (Behavior 20)** (High). The routine is the body of hero state 47 (climb up onto a ledge while rising), so it is hero code: the earlier claim that no hero path reaches it is withdrawn. The second state word's value 5 is the air phase 5 written after the routine sets state 7 when the hero is not rising (touched with the vertical velocity above 0 gives air phase 0); its "animation 7" is that state number; the owner class is the hero. Test vectors: 110 and 169.

- Resolved: **Landing recovery (Behavior 20).** There is no landing recovery: the 120 is a grab cooldown write of the climb sub-modes (hero word +0x9CC), counted down once per hero update (Behavior 20).
- Resolved: **Homing approach (Behavior 21).** It is hero state 35 (climb up onto a ledge from a fall), called by the state dispatch; 0.5 is a per-tick facing turn rate (the position moves a fixed 0.25 per tick along the unit vector to the target) and 0.95 and 0.75 are fixed constants in the code, not data words. The write of air phase 4 with request 76 is the walk-off fall (Behavior 9A), not an entry to this routine. It belongs to hero locomotion, not to SPEC-0036 (Behavior 21; High).
- Resolved: **Stance routines (Behavior 4A): codes and helpers** (High). The helper for state 39 is the stance-counter reset (R-0039). The helper for state 1 is the threat sense that sets the 40-tick guard timer (SPEC-0036, Guard timer and per-attacker block), followed by the hero idle picker; the codes are 63 (guard raised, held until the timer runs out) and the idle picker's ids, all listed in Behavior 4A.1. The remaining codes of the stance routine are 2 to 11 and 106 (states 2 to 6), 87 (state 22), 82 (state 39) and 85 or 106 (state 40), and its walk-off exit requests 70 or 76. Test vector: Behavior 4A.1, guard timer.
- Resolved: **Stance and stick gates.** In the stance routine (states 1 to 6, 22, 39 and 40) the walk-off values 3.0 and -3.0 and the motion length 0.01 are constants written into the code. In the shield guard (state 20) the 0.95 facing test is one too. In the hang state (state 30) the 0.25 and -0.25 along-edge tests, the 0.95 progress gates and the 0.5 steering rate are constants as well, and so are the 1.0, 0.5 and 0.33 split values of the speed tiers. None of these has a data word. The only data words read by the stance, tier, guard and hang routines are in the speed tiers: the run re-entry hold 0x2DD7D8, the stop threshold 0x2DD7DC (0.1) and the smallest moving speed value 0x2DD7E0 (0.3) (Behavior 5.1; Where it lives). The word 0x2DD7C8 (-0.25) is read only by the hang-and-leap state (state 28). [High]
- Resolved: **Target record and action ids.** The action-test ids for states 19, 30, 35 and 47 are resolved above. State 18 tests 41, 42, 144 and 145 (Behavior 4A.2). State 49 (feed a petpet) requests 138 first and goes on when that clip passes progress 0.99. State 58 (climb onto a block) requests 134 in sub-mode 0 and moves to sub-mode 2; sub-mode 2 tests 134 and finishes at progress 0.95. The current action word is the id last passed to the hero's animation request method, the call behind every "request N" in this spec: the request stores the id, the second id and a third value, and clears the clip play counter. The word is -1 when the hero object is created and 0 after the hero's setup. [High]

- Resolved: **State-13 exit and stick sign.** The exit to state 20 (Behavior 3C) has no stick test: sub-state 3 always arms it, sub-state 4 arms it on a frame with event bit 0x40, and it fires with a shield-family entry and L1 held or pressed (SPEC-0036 conflict C1, resolved). For Tor sub-states 7, 8 and 10, the left-stick sign and byte mapping are resolved in Behavior 3C: low Y is forward and low X is left after conversion and negation (High).
- Resolved: **Pad byte mapping.** Left stick Y = +0x11F, left X = +0x123, right Y = +0x127, right X = +0x12B in the pad record. +0x11F is the Y (forward) byte read by Behaviors 2 and 3C (High for the byte layout; Medium-High for the Y and X roles in gameplay, which the ladder climb and the stick-forward exits of Behavior 51 agree with).
- Answered: grab-point hang entry (Behavior 9). Only the second copy of the predicate in the selector's tail stays open (Low).
- **Cross-held hang offset** (Behavior 9): Resolved: while animation 113 plays, the 3.0-unit adjustment changes the Y lane of the locomotion sub-object's +0x270 vector each tick; the other lanes are unchanged. The vector's broader semantic role remains unknown. **[High for lane; Low for vector role]**
- Answered: bounce writer. The air landing phase on a ground record of kind 17 or 18 (Behavior 10).
- Answered: climb sub-modes 19 and above do nothing (Behavior 51, state 21).
- Resolved: climb sub-modes 3 and 17 after a panel hand-off. Sub-mode 3 backs the hero off an overhang to at least 1.3 from the wall and makes the panel above the target; sub-mode 17 hangs the hero from the top edge, shuffles along it, climbs over (sub-mode 7) or returns to the free climb (Behavior 51, Top-edge hang) (High).
- Resolved (R-0085, in part): sub-mode 1 uses N/V/U panel projections and has no single world-space arrival point. Its outer approach branch uses n = N·P > 1.5: when grounded it faces -N at rate 0.5; with ground gap at least 0.25 it steps 0.1 along B3. The close branches apply ordered panel-relative corrections and retain the distinct action-121 completion routes; the exact tests and adjustment order are in Behavior 51. Its explicit exits include h <= LV - 0.5 or h < -H to sub-mode 0, and B3·N > 0 with h > LV - 0.5 to sub-mode 10. The full visible approach path is not play-verified. Resolved (High): sub-mode 4's ahead/behind tests are B3·NQ > 0.75 and B3·NQ < -0.75; movement checks stay on T until Q is promoted. Sub-modes 11/12 apply -0.75 B3 before target promotion. Sub-mode 17 tests the camera-relative stick vector projected onto T's U and N bases and returns if either projected component is exactly zero; raw camera-forward input can still proceed when its side projection is nonzero. Its state-4 exit requires event bit 0x40 plus f > 0 before progress 0.95. Sub-mode 18 applies the one-shot N·P - H position correction and sends sub-mode 4 on the same target T.
- Resolved: **State-13 lookups.** The two lookups of the state-20 exit are the hero's shield-family entry and its empty test: the exit fires when the entry exists and is not empty (SPEC-0021; SPEC-0036, state 13).
- Resolved: **Button chain, second argument.** It is the busy flag: 1 when the hero's busy-state predicate is true or the game status is above 0. When busy, the L2, L1, Cross and Square routes take no action; Cross and Square still end the chain on a press; L2 never ends the chain (Behavior 1A, rule 5a). High.
- Resolved: **hard and soft landings.** Air phase 10 (request 118) and air phase 6 (request 68) are the hard and soft landings of the jump states (Behavior 50), not a guard or an evade. No button starts them. The soft-landing code contains a Cross chain branch, but its 0x40 event window never opens for either hero with the disc animation data (SPEC-0005).
- Answered: lock-on. No hero-side lock-on; L3 calls only menu helpers (Behavior 1A).
- Resolved: **Hero swap.** An L1 and R1 chord switches to the other hero (SPEC-0053 section 3.8 c). It is tested in the front-end in-play handler after the hero's button chain in the same pass (Behavior 1). The chain may move the hero to state 20 on the L1 press; the switch-ready gate accepts state 20, so the chord still passes (High for the order; Medium for the consequence).
- Answered: **Start and Select.** The hero button handler has no direct Start or Select action. The front-end handler opens the item screen on Start and the pause page on Select, subject to the busy gate; loss of usable pad slot 0 also opens the pause page (SPEC-0021, Panel visibility and clears, item 6). This is the menu path, not a hero action-state transition.
- Resolved: **Latch words.** The 0xF0F0 mask is set while quick-item panel A (0x2DCCC4) or panel B (0x2DCCF0) is open and for one pass after (0x2DD038 follows them one pass late) (Behavior 1A, rule 3). High.
- Resolved: **Quick-item panel helpers.** The panel step runs after the state routine, outside the button chain, while flag D and the game-state code are zero (rule 7). Up or Down with both panels closed opens panel A or panel B behind the in-play gate; Triangle closes an open panel A and, with both closed, runs the use step; Circle selects and closes; Cross selects, closes and uses. There is no Down-only cue: every direction press plays cue 0x9A. The use step is the item-use dispatch of SPEC-0021 (force 1, notify 1, gate 1), refused for an empty marker or a dead hero (Behavior 1A). High.
- Resolved: **gates of the L1 and Cross branches** (High). L1 needs a usable shield in equipment slot 2 and a hero that is not busy; it ends the chain when pressed or held (Behavior 1A, rules 5 and 5a). Cross needs a hero that is not busy, a Cross press edge and a ground normal with a vertical part of at least 0.82; the block-edge grab test then decides state 19 instead of the jump (Behavior 50). The selector never writes state 27: the Rope Gun entry is made by the charge routine of state 13 when the aim mode is on, an anchor is selected and R2 is held (State 27 in Behavior 51; Behavior 1A, button table). Test vectors: 164 and 168 (ground normal 0.82 jumps, 0.81 does not); 170 (state 27 entry).
- Resolved in part (R-0087, clause 4): the state-27 behavior-record gate, phase-3 landing flag and a qualifying hit callback all call the shared rope reset. The action selector also resets it in state 27, sub-mode 4, on a fresh Square press. A broad hero virtual-update method calls the reset unconditionally as one step in its fixed per-tick sequence; the upstream virtual dispatch gate and cadence remain unknown. The hit callback resets before attacker-specific processing, so release does not by itself prove damage.
- Resolved: **states 22 and 19.** State 22 is the sneak idle (Square held with no target); state 19 is the hang from a block's top edge (Behavior 51).
- Resolved: **Behavior 4B caller.** It is the body of state 29 (hold a door), which is never entered in play.
- Resolved: **Behavior 4B scale and step.** The scale is the hero radius r (1.175), and 0.075 is a positional step along the facing, not a speed.
- Answered: Behavior 4B codes are moot, since state 29 is never entered (Behavior 51).
- Resolved: **Behavior 4B dead-zone byte.** Moot: state 29 is never entered in play.
- Resolved: **Behavior 4B record.** The record claimed on the first tick is the door, taken from the motion record's door word.
- **Displacement scale word 0x2DD6C8.** Resolved (High): the word has exactly five readers in the boot program, all inside the two hero attack step tables (Tor's and Roberta's state-13 stick steps). The door-hold body of Behavior 4B does not read it; it uses the hero radius and its own fixed step instead.
- Answered: **Behavior 23 owner.** The right-stick state and reticle fields are the hero aim mode in SPEC-0036, not generic character look channels; its 0–4 mode is passed to the separate aim-view object while camera mode is 0 (SPEC-0027, Right-stick aim mode and the camera).
- Answered: **Behavior 23 tick length.** Start-up writes the runtime tick length as 1/60 second and the frame counter advances once per main-loop pass (SPEC-0007, Per-tick words and One pass of the main loop). The aim update counts ticks; see SPEC-0036, Right-stick aim mode. The precise writers of each aim target field are covered there.
- Resolved: Behavior 23 mode routines. Modes 0 to 2 reset the marker scale to 0.3, the spin rate to 0.8 and (modes 1 and 2) the colour to (0.33, 0, 0, 1); modes 3 and 4 start a 0.08 s pop (scale 2.0 to 0.2, colour alpha 0.1 to 1.0, red for 3 and green for 4) and spin at 4.0. The constants of modes 3 and 4 tune that pop (Behavior 23). High for the writes; the marker is never drawn because its model is absent.
- Resolved: Behavior 23 channels. They are not a look yaw and pitch or a lean: the two angles are the marker model's spin angles (only the first moves), the two values are its placement slopes in the camera frame (both stay 0) and the clamped 0.1 to 40 value is its distance in front of the camera (stays 10.0). See Behavior 23 for the placement formula and the test vector. High for the field roles; Medium-High for the camera row roles.
- Behavior 23: the clamp limits 0.1 and 40.0 have no known data address; they are treated as fixed constants.
- Resolved: Behavior 30 uses 0x2DD6A8 as a candidate-distance range in its hero target scan; Behavior 9 uses 0x2DD7BC as the maximum side offset from the hero to another grab line. The equal 20.0 values do not implement the same filter. Still open: the meaning of the 100.0 range (state 1), limit 90 and 0.5 angle parameter.
- Answered: Behavior 24 is the push-into-face counter of the block fit test (Behavior 15).
- Resolved: Behavior 25 is the passive health and magic regeneration of the hero; the total is the pool maximum and the flag selects the health rate (2.0) or the magic rate (1.0) (Behavior 25; SPEC-0036, Regeneration). High.
- Resolved: Behavior 27 is the hero's impact sound cue selector, not an animation selector: the codes 170, 171 and 186 to 189 are sound cue numbers and the count is the armour id of the equipment list (SPEC-0031, hero impact cues). See the corrected Behavior 27. High.
- Resolved: Behavior 28 is the recoil material to sound cue map of the sword bounce; the result is played on the hero's own sound bank in attack sub-state 13 (Behavior 28; SPEC-0028, Blade recoil). High.
- Answered: Behavior 29 is the D-pad pressure virtual stick; its derived output has no gameplay reader. The 127 centre belongs to this path and the 127.5 centre to Behavior 2.
- Resolved in part: Behavior 26 state exclusions are current hero states 25, 33, 51 and 55; after the height-plus-radius comparison fails, the fallback requires a successful candidate-type table lookup and the strict relation 0.4 × candidate field +0x94 < candidate field +0x98. The intended meaning of the 25.0 height limit and fallback fields remains open.
- Resolved: Behavior 32 uses the motion record ground-normal Y at +0x14 (High). The 0.82 cutoff (inclusive) gates Cross jumping: a slope up to 34.9 degrees. The 0.85 cutoff (inclusive) is used by the air ledge reach predicate: a slope up to 31.8 degrees, and the predicate also needs the object-wall flag clear and a ledge touched this tick. 0.85 also appears separately as a hero-height multiplier in the climb-up class. The reach predicate adds 0.75 times hero height unless the jumping-state predicate is true; the hang-class test has its own additive 0.75 clearance. The values are fixed constants with no data word; no further design reason is recoverable from the program. Test vectors: 164 (ground-normal Y 0.82 passes and 0.81 fails; 0.85 meets the reach cutoff) and 168.
- Answered: **Behavior 31's 2.8-unit spawn is not the state-57 carry.** State 57 only attaches, carries and releases its target; no spawn or throw occurs in that routine (Behavior 18; G4L movement follow-up). Resolved: its caller is the feed-a-petpet state 49, which drops the food 2.8 units ahead (Behavior 31; High).
- Behavior 34 and 35: the meaning of the states and codes; the handling of queue kinds other than 0 to 3; the width of the queue's count. Resolved in part: the four-stage sequence of Behavior 35 is the phase machine of hero state 38 (High). The remaining Behavior 35 bullets and the deferred queue are still open.
- Resolved: Frame counter writer (0x368F98) and tick-length writers for the gates of Behaviors 24 and 25. The pass counter has one writer, the end of every main-loop pass (+1), and the tick length is written once at start-up (SPEC-0007, Clock; SPEC-0036, Regeneration). High.
- Owner of the three 15-slot target lists (search, insert, remove and swap by index, and a predicate on one list). Not described in this spec. Candidate owners: this spec (its callers are here) or the pet-list spec SPEC-0021.
- Answered: **Visual sub-object binders.** Their fixed-resource bindings and hero-relative placement are specified in SPEC-0040, Behavior 28 (Hero visual sub-object binders); this is character display setup, not locomotion.
- Resolved: **hint icon owner.** The routine once described as a hint-icon chooser is not a prompt or HUD routine. For the hero it reads the selected entry of the weapon family list (category 1) and of the shield family list (category 2) for that hero and turns each entry's model id into a held-model slot value: the weapon slot gets id minus 98 (id minus 103 when the id is above 110), the shield slot gets id minus 106 when that is 0 to 4, otherwise none (-1). It then writes those two slot values to the hero's held-model slots. It belongs to SPEC-0021 (held weapon and shield models; SPEC-0046, Held items and character attachments). The interaction prompts are specified in SPEC-0022 (Interaction prompts) and the D-pad hint icon in SPEC-0023 section 9 (High).
- Answered: **Area change queue.** Pending world slots and their transition processing belong to SPEC-0006 (Areas, World buffers and transitions).
- Answered: **Event queue insert.** The referenced routine is the item ask-about hook and its once-only interaction task, covered by SPEC-0030; it is not a generic clock/event-queue operation.
- Behavior 36: Resolved for the latch (High): it is never cleared in the boot program and its only reader is the L3 and R3 test that opens the debug watch screen, so it gates nothing in title, menu or play entry. The main loop checks the sequence in ordinary title, front-end/menu and in-play states serviced by that loop; nested blocking routines suspend the check. Other blocking flows, disconnected second-pad behavior and the design intent remain open. The separate “Positions unlocked” routine has no static caller and is treated as unreachable. The watch screen is specified in SPEC-0008.
- Resolved: **edge test, later gates** (Behavior 37). The facing gate uses the forward row and the locomotion direction; the 5.0 is a five-tick look-ahead of the motion, not a start height, and the 0.5 offsets move the reference point along the forward row for negative types. Behavior 15 is the climb-onto-block fit test over movable blocks, not an edge test, so the two are unrelated.
- Resolved: **Behavior 38 second store.** The second store only sets w = 1; the result is P' = 2 P0 - P, the tick's root step reversed. The routine is the edge guard of generic characters (SPEC-0040, Behavior 14E) and never fires for the heroes.
- **Ground-record test of the pre-step** (Behavior 38). Resolved: it is the shared motion-record pre-step (SPEC-0040, Behavior 14E), and its ground-record tests are the hero hazard rules of SPEC-0057 (Ground hazards): the 2.3 threshold is the height of a linked overlay above the hero that kills both heroes, the helper that runs when it is exceeded is that double kill, and the timed call is the 10 HP hurting-surface hit once every 2.5 s of game clock. The runtime word used by the timed call is game time in seconds, initialized to zero and advanced by dt once at the end of each pass (SPEC-0007, One pass of the main loop). High.
- Resolved: **Stop test flag and move routine** (Behavior 39; High for the arithmetic). The stop test is the grounded test of the motion-record force step, the per-record factor is the record weight, the flag at +0x14C is the clear-on-ground flag (set to 1 only by two enemy and NPC controllers; heroes never set it), the move routines are the friction coefficient and the friction step, and the clamp limits friction to the current speed (Behavior 39 and SPEC-0040, Behavior 14A, item 5c). Test vector: Behavior 39 (grounded record, speed 3 loses 0.64 to give 2.36; speed 0.3 stops).
- **Probe selection** (resolved for the recovered routine; Behavior 40): after a miss at the current point, the second probe uses the one-tick advanced position, with the half carried-motion offset when that advanced point is more than 0.1 from its anchor. Both probes use the same point-versus-oriented-box helper and 0.75 clearance; a hit is passed to the contact-response routine. The fixed-0.75 routine's owner and broader caller set remain open.
- Resolved: **Behavior 41 target.** The target is the Draik breath beam's 3-D length, not a heading (SPEC-0029).
- Resolved: **Showcase pose task: weighted-call purpose** (Behavior 42). The routine is the item-showcase pose task (SPEC-0021, Item showcase), not a hero-state chooser: its record holds the collected item id and the item's showcase text. The weighted call is the timed hold (LoopHeroAnim form) that the task uses for the default pose when the item has text: the hero holds the pose for 1.0 s while the text box opens, then the task re-issues the hold each frame for at least 2.0 s and until the box closes. The name passed to the display helper titles that text box (Tor or Roberta). Values 6, 7 and 8 are stinger effect indices, not sound-block cue ids or animation blend priorities; they select VAG resources 262, 263 and 264 (SPEC-0009, Effect and stinger index; SPEC-0024, Table 14). The audible contents of the three VAG resources are not described here. [High]
- Resolved: **Push share** (Behavior 43). There is no share. The other party is a placed object, not an actor: the routine writes only the character, which takes the whole contact displacement after being carried through the object's current and rest blocks. The rest block (+0xC0 group) is disc data; the current block (+0x100 group) is built from it at load and rewritten by every animation update; the double turn of the residual is the exact inverse of the carry's two turns when the object is in its rest orientation at scale 1; the push has one call site, in the ground-contact step, which is reached from nine places (Behavior 43, Callers, with test vectors from a real object). **[High for writers, roles and callers; Medium for the intent of the double turn]**
- Resolved: **stick axis order** (Behavior 45). +0x11F is the left-stick Y and +0x123 the X (High): the pad refresh copies raw packet bytes 4, 5, 6 and 7 to +0x12B, +0x127, +0x123 and +0x11F in that order, so +0x11F holds byte 7 (left Y) and +0x123 byte 6 (left X); the right-stick invert flags act on +0x12B and +0x127, which fixes the order. A raw Y below 127 is the stick pushed up. The helpers of Behavior 45 divide each byte by 87.5 after the dead-zone conversion of Behavior 2 and apply no sign flip, so a pushed-up stick gives a negative forward value there; the ladder reads it as climbing up (Behavior 51). Test vector: bytes X = 127, Y = 40 give v = 40 − 127.5 = −87.5, conversion −47.5, forward value −0.543.
- Resolved: **stick blend branches** (Behavior 45). The stated reading is right: in sub-mode 4 the helpers return the scaled raw byte, and in other sub-modes the dot product with the caller's vector.
- **Random timer caller** (resolved in part; Behavior 46). The writer is the bush-instance reset path, and its per-instance update reads the stored value only as a zero/nonzero branch gate; the direct reader does not decrement it or inspect its magnitude. Why the bush retains this positive randomized value and whether indirect writers affect the gate remain open. The time base 60 is taken from word 0x368FA0 (see Where it lives).
- Resolved: **Animated-object sound slot inputs and duration** (Behavior 47). The +0x1E4 frame-count record is the animated placed object selected by AddAnimObjSound's object index. Slot field 0 references that object record, field 1 stores the cue id, and fields 2 and 3 store the cue's start and end times after fraction scaling by (F − 1)/15 seconds (SPEC-0030 row 224; SPEC-0031, Animated-object sounds; SPEC-0028, Animated-object record).
- **Hash draw callers** (Behavior 48). Resolved (partly): the hash draw has one caller, a periodic effect counter that passes its own step count as the seed; that counter has no caller or table entry, so the hash draw is not part of play, and every draw of play uses the game's generator (SPEC-0008). Still open: whether some unlisted indirect path reaches the counter.
- **State 30 extra routine** (resolved in part; Behavior 44). After clearing +0x9AC and requesting state 1 or 22, the exit dispatches the class-specific routine with mode 0 and arguments -1, -1. Its behavior and purpose remain open.
- **Hero travel writer.** Resolved: the motion-record step moves the hero by the clip root step (Behavior 5.2).
- Resolved: **Gravity.** The value 32.0 is in the physics block, written at start-up. In the air the hero's vertical velocity changes by 32 G / 30 per tick and the height by the vertical velocity / 30, equal to 128 G units per second squared (SPEC-0040, Behavior 14A, item 7).
- Resolved: **Fall law to implement.** The clean side implements the record law of SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A item 7: v += (F / W) k, F = 0, p += v k with k = 1/30, gravity adding minus W times 32 times G to the force; from rest the drop after K ticks is 0.0177778 G K (K + 1). After 10 ticks at gain 1.0 the drop is 1.9556. The leap of state 28 keeps its own 0.02 per tick.
- Resolved: **run gate writer** (Behavior 4). Only zero is ever written, on hero set-up and on the climb-entry reset; nothing else touches the word, so running means R1 is held (High).
- Resolved: **Event flag 0x40 inventory** (Behavior 1A, 4A.3). It is an event-window bit of the current action, not an action identifier; the shield-guard steering rules test action 64 and progress separately. Action ids with at least one 0x40 frame in their mapped clip are Tor main: 13, 14, 15, 16, 21, 22, 27, 31, 59, 71, 75, 118, 134; Tor sub: 14, 15, 16, 27; Tor face: 14, 15, 16, 27; Roberta main: 16, 75; Roberta sub: 16, 118; Roberta face: 16, 118. The event query spans the four playback records (main layers 0 and 1, sub-part and face), so a listed family can satisfy a queried window (SPEC-0005, Table B frame bits). Neither hero has a 0x40 frame on action 68 in any family, so its chained-jump and L1 windows never open in this build. The clean side must not read the event bit as "action 0x40 is current".
- Resolved: the shield-guard steer call is the shared character facing-blend method; its vector argument is a desired facing direction, not a movement vector. The centered-stick path supplies the locomotion facing, and the non-centered path supplies the normalized stick direction. At weight 1.0 it stores the requested facing, with a small side-axis offset when directions are almost opposite (High for the shared method, Medium-High for this call mapping). The moving flag is read by the shared per-pass facing update as a gate for its moving-facing branch above speed 0.01. The recovered direct writer census shows multiple action routines setting it to one; no zero store was found in those recovered routines. This does not establish the complete indirect consumer set or intended design label (Medium-High).
- Resolved: **Slope and step for the hero** (Medium for the summary, High for each piece; the summary is in Behavior 6, Hero slope and step summary: root-step projection with no slope limit, a one-tick step up of at most 2.5 that nothing blocks, a 0.82 jump gate and a 0.85 ledge gate, and the 0.75 check for block moves only). Earlier detail: Hero placement uses the shared ground-attach routine with a height-plus-2.5 probe ceiling. The attach refreshes the ground normal before that tick's root travel, and travel projects against that sampled normal; on a slope, the projected step can therefore include vertical displacement (Behavior 8; vector 156). The separate 0.75 height-step comparison gates the state-18 movable-block check; it is not called by ordinary state-32 stick locomotion, which applies animation root motion. This does not rule out other dynamically dispatched uses of the shared check or settle terrain/contact behavior after the root step (vector 157).
- Resolved: **block list and edge guard** (Behaviors 15 and 38). The 0xB0-byte records are the movable blocks (Behavior 15); the second store gives P' = 2 P0 - P (SPEC-0040, Behavior 14E).
- Resolved: **motion-record step, zeroing test naming** (Behavior 5.2 and 5.5). The character's vector is the steering target of the locomotion sub-object (the stick direction while the stick is not centred), and the exemptions 21, 23 and 41 and the case 53 are action-state tests (High). Still open (Low): whether characters other than the heroes reach this step with an enabled locomotion sub-object.
- Resolved: **ledge-drop test** (Behavior 49). The edge height h(p) comes from the edge's top plane and height value; the side gate uses face-plane values of the reference point and the position; the 5.0 check is the look-ahead of Behavior 37 without its c >= a part. The callers are the hero's wall contacts (Behavior 49, Callers). Behavior 15 is the climb-onto-block fit test and is unrelated. It shares the record fields, the reference point offsets and the look-ahead with Behavior 37 and runs before it.
- Resolved: **Strafe jump sides** (Behavior 50). State 43 is the jump to the hero's right, 44 to the left (Behavior 11; Medium-High).
- Resolved: **State 11 writer** (Behavior 50). Both edge-contact routines write it in state 6 at an accepted ledge-drop edge with facing dot normal at most 0, when the contacted frame-record or type-10 edge record has type other than 1 or 6. This branch does not test the hero's current ground entry. The separate kind-1-or-6 gate belongs to the states-3/4 offer branch, which reads the current ground-query entry; the nearby kind-2 offer test uses that same current entry, while the kind-2 ledge-drop shortcut uses the candidate from a new look-ahead probe (Behavior 49). Resolved: the launch uses the facing (forward row) times -10; the stored direction is not read (High).
- **Air phase details** (Behavior 50). Resolved: phase 5 runs the landing choice, then the bounce test, then the block-edge test, on every phase-5 tick; the hard landing leaves the phase at 10 and writes the state field directly (High). Resolved in part: an additional event-window handler checks a Cross press edge, requests a state-7 jump and calls the normal chooser using the locomotion sub-object, as do the soft-landing chain and ladder jump. The handler has no direct code-call caller or confirmed registration in the local static references, so its reachability remains open. The alternate strafe chooser is called by the Cross route and soft-landing chain. Resolved: Tor's 1.5 plunge height gate is a fixed constant, not a data word (High).
- Resolved: **Tor's air slash** (Behavior 50). It never happens in states 7 to 11 and never enters state 13. It exists only in the strafe jumps 43 to 46: armed by the attack input in air phases 2 to 5, started by a release before 12 held ticks or by an R2 press, and kept in the strafe-jump state with sub-state 11 and second main id 25 until landing. It deals no damage (High).
- Resolved: **Sub-state 11 left over after a slash** (Behavior 50; High for the recovered routes). The moving landing of a strafe jump writes state 32 directly and leaves sub-state 11 in place, and the state setter by itself does not reset that word. On an accepted ordinary attack-selector route, however, the same update requests state 13 and then the attack-start router writes the initial sub-state from the gesture mode before the state-13 routine can run: Tor's values 0–3 select sub-states 1, 9, 7 or 8; Roberta's select 1 or 5, 8, 6 or 7. Therefore this normal entry does not consume the stale Tor slash value. Other recovered entries also supply their own value: Roberta's air shots enter with 11 or 12, and the world-press trigger sets Tor's sub-state to 13. Tor's slash itself keeps the strafe-jump state; state 23 has no ordinary route in shipped data. This closes the stale-value question for recovered entry paths, not unlisted external state writes.
- Resolved: **Second-id progress with no second id** (Behavior 50; High). A second id of -1 makes the progress test search the four playback records for clip index -1. No record ever holds -1 (idle records hold clip 0 and every started clip has an index of 0 or more), so the test is false and the air-attack flag is not ended early. An air attack therefore does not end when a soft landing starts; it ends when the soft-landing clip reaches progress 0.99 (the end of phase 6 clears the flag together with the air phase), and the hit scan keeps running meanwhile with no attack window to find. Test vector: the soft-landing vector under Behavior 50, air phase 6.
- Resolved: **Climb and ladder grabs from the air.** The ceiling-panel grab (state 21, sub-mode 18), the wall-panel grab (sub-mode 1) and the ladder grab (state 41, with the height window) are Behaviors 22 and 22A.
- **Traversal bodies** (Behavior 51). States 26 (statue) and 29 (door) are unreachable in play (resolved); their bodies stay partly described. The sidle (state 23) is never entered in play (resolved); its body stays Medium. State 30's action-test ids are resolved: 72 in sub-mode 1, 61 then 60 in sub-modes 3, 5 and 6. No 0.75 threshold is known for sub-mode 3; its tests are the 1.5 distance checks, while the 0.25 along-component checks are in sub-modes 5 and 6. Sub-mode 1 of state 58 remains without an ordinary entry route. Resolved: the target heights of state 19 sub-mode 1 and state 58 sub-mode 0 are the block top minus 3.7 and minus 1.2, reached by a per-tick straight step of 0.5 and 0.2 (Behavior 51). State 47's sub-mode-0 target is 0.5 outward along the edge record's first-axis offset from its face plane, with a height 1.35 below the geometric top plane (Behavior 51). Resolved: the state-19 shimmy limits are measured along the block face from its end on the hero's right: 61 needs along above 1.0 and 60 needs along below s − 1.0, with s the block size (Behavior 51).
- Resolved in part (R-0080). (1) The offer, hang and climb inequalities and their strict/inclusive boundaries are given in Behaviors 32 and 51; the drop tests use ground-probe height g, so y, t and H alone are insufficient without the hero-to-ground gap. (2) The 0.25 in h(p) is a vertical top-plane offset, not a horizontal face-distance test. (3) The chooser consumes one held edge record and walks no edge list; the first candidate depends on contact-list traversal. Later offers replace the held edge only for a strictly greater facing-alignment score; equal scores preserve the current edge. (4) State 30, 35 and 47 target plane offsets and heights are specified in Behavior 51. The state-30 hang target uses signed plane distance d−0.7. Pack 909 has paired examples where adjacent collision boxes lie on the negative-normal side, but the nearby candidate frame record at the lower ladder has a box whose full bounds lie on its positive-normal side. Since the actual held frame is unidentified, the sign of the rendered-solid side and whether the 0.7 offset moves into or away from the wall remain frame-specific and unresolved. (5) State 37 phase 1 compares the signed projection on the held edge’s first basis (the plane-normal basis) with its separate run-length scalar; this decision coordinate is not the run-axis position, and the run basis itself is not read for this decision. The helper requests action 2 and faces the positive first basis but writes no position directly. After the projection exceeds 0.15, a value at or below the stored length completes the phase, while a value beyond the length can follow authored link 1 and complete it. The later negative-projection branch associated with link 3 remains unconfirmed because its projection input is not established. Actual travel comes from root motion and can be changed by the transition tick's prior action sample and later contact/ground corrections; under ordinary face-on selection, the steady positive-forward action-2 component points along +normal and away from the wall (Medium-High). For the lower-ladder candidate record 164/5, local side aligns with its positive run basis: Tor's local-x root sample contributes approximately −0.01514R to +0.01563R along-run per sampled key (key 0: +0.01417R if newly started), while Roberta's local-x is zero at all 20 keys. These are conditional root contributions, not net travel; the actual held record, cursor, play rate, contact correction and projection-versus-run-length endpoint rule remain open. The state-37 request happens after root motion on its contact frame, so it cannot alter that frame's displacement. The next tick runs phase 0 before motion and animation; if phase 0 advances to phase 1, that motion still uses the previous action's sample. On the following tick phase 1 requests action 2 before motion, but motion still uses the previous sample. The animation pass then evaluates action 2 and saves its root sample, which is first used by motion one tick later. The exact sample values depend on the live action and clip cursor; the call order is established. Its phase-2 turn uses the normalized 0.25 target / 0.75 current-facing blend, with a side-axis correction when the headings are nearly opposite. (6) State-30 shimmy uses the run-plane position value s and edge length L with the listed 0.25/0/L comparisons. Its camera-relative stick vector is projected onto the held edge run basis: a negative projection requests action 61, a positive projection requests action 60, and zero selects neither branch. This settles input sign relative to the edge; global screen-left/right and collision-corrected travel remain dependent on the held record, camera pose, animation root sample and contact response. Ground-kind predicates read the selected type-5 ground record's kind field at +0x38; kind and the separate surface field at +0x40 are distinct. The kind-1-or-6 and edge-offer-not-2 checks use the current ground record, while the ledge-drop kind-2 shortcut reads a separate look-ahead probe result. State-11 selection uses the contacted edge type rather than the current ground entry. Pack 909 has kind-1 and kind-6 populated grids near the farm, but no kind-2 type-5 records; the full probe winner at the lower-ladder target is not established. The semantic class or owner of kinds 1, 2 and 6 remains unknown.
- Resolved: **Block mover** (Behavior 4A.2). n = (clip key count - whole part of the cursor - 2), doubled only for the push clip 42. Behavior 4B is the door hold, not the block mover. Resolved: the move window uses the layer-0 cursor over its key count (Medium-High; test vector 123).
- Resolved: **Owners of Behaviors 17, 20 and 21.** The jump-action helpers are the climb corner sub-modes of state 21, the three-state air routine is hero state 47 (Behavior 20) and the three-state homing is hero state 35 (Behavior 21), all reached from the hero's state dispatch; the 120-tick value is the grab cooldown of the climb sub-modes (Behavior 20) (High). Their owner is the hero in every case, so nothing is open here.
- Resolved: **Flight curve** (Behavior 50). +0x60 is the velocity and +0x70 the force (SPEC-0040, Open question 176). A standing jump peaks 3.2028 above take-off at tick 24, decides its landing at tick 47 and touches the ground at tick 49.
- Resolved: **floor-contact flag on landings** (Behavior 50). It is set by near-horizontal contacts (|n_y| > 0.9) and does not shorten landings on probe-visible floors; it matters over surfaces the probe misses and after ceiling hits (Medium).
- Resolved: **strafe and plunge curves** (Behaviors 11 and 50). The strafe flight is in Behavior 11; the plunge flight (root motion of clip 23 with gravity off, then a forced -8.0 descent) is in Behavior 50 and test vector 134.
- Answered: **Edge-offer boost height** (Behavior 49). The stored value is the vertical clearance d computed by the edge acceptance test of Behavior 37, plus 0.75; it is not the along-edge distance. SPEC-0018, edge acceptance value, confirms this use.
- Answered: Behavior 15 uses the box top as loaded for its fit test. A push changes only the block's horizontal position; its displacement is along one of four horizontal directions, and no ground-height resample occurs during the push. The 0.75 limit gates the move by comparing two ground samples at the front/hero target. Ground height is sampled again during placement or re-placement (SPEC-0048, Movable blocks; High). Whether any reached on-disc push crosses uneven terrain while passing the gate remains open.
- Open (R-0010): the screen side of L (up cross forward) is inferred from SPEC-0027's free-camera rule. A check against the game's screen is still needed before the sign is final.
- Resolved (R-0106 clauses 1–2; High for the branch and gate): the hero dispatcher clears the motion record's no-ground-snap request (+0x148) every tick. State 30 sets it only in sub-mode 4, after the obstruction/attachment predicate is nonzero and before the optional forward-stick exit; the request applies for that tick only. The state-30 early gate uses the shared ground-gap result: it is zero when floor-contact (+0x150) is set, otherwise it is the motion record's +0x24 value. Results at or below zero return from every sub-mode except 4. This is not a separate ledge-gap field. The other direct hero-state writers are: state 21 phase 7 while action 0x3B is active below progress 0.95; state 41 phase 8 while action 0x87 is active below 0.95; state 47 phases 1/2 after the actor-ground gate passes while action 0x86 is active below 0.95; state 56 phases 1/2 during timed vertical adjustment while action 0x44 is requested; and state 58 phase 2 while action 0x86 is active below 0.95. The hero dispatcher clears the request before each handler, so each is a one-tick override repeated on qualifying ticks. A separate region-character updater clears its own field each update and state 16 writes it unconditionally; this does not describe hero state 16. These conditions establish the request writes, not the visible amount of ground correction suppressed in every contact case. [High]
- Resolved (R-0017 1 and 3). (1) The shield guard's dot test compares two unit vectors: the camera-relative stick direction (the stick's forward and side values turned by the camera's forward and right basis vectors, Behavior 45) and the hero's stored locomotion facing. It is neither the camera's forward alone nor the raw stick. Below 0.95 it requests animation 119 (Medium-High). (3) A strike that forces the blocked flag (0x20) does not send the sequencer anywhere: the take-hit with the blocked flag set writes no state (state 4 is the run state, not a block state), and it plays the block reaction (shield cue 0, then request 0x40) in whatever state the hero is in, unless the hero is busy (SPEC-0036, Taking a hit, step 5; High). It is the same path as a guarded block. Test: a 0x20 strike of 15 on a hero at 100 HP in state 1 gives health 85, no state change and the block reaction. **[High for (3); Medium-High for (1)]**
